**Real gap found and fixed (2026-08-17)**: the mechanism's own proof had silently gone stale. `bootstrap/secrets/ntfy-webhook-url.enc.yaml` was last committed 2026-08-08, but the live Secret was recreated fresh during backlog #49's 2026-08-10 rebuild — confirmed via the Secret's own `creationTimestamp`. `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`'s own rebuild account already recorded why, just never connected back to this item: `sops`/`age` weren't installed in that environment, so the rebuild's `sops_value()` run hit its documented hard-exit path (enc file exists but tooling doesn't — refuses to guess) rather than silently recovering, and the owner chose a fresh topic over installing recovery tooling mid-rebuild. The encrypted file was never regenerated to match. **Losing the laptop today would not have actually recovered the real live topic** — the exact failure mode ADR 0034 exists to prevent, quietly true for a week. Fixed (`platform`#190): the live value extracted read-only via `kubectl` (the running Secret itself untouched, no rotation, no disruption) and re-encrypted with the existing public recipient key — decrypting the stale file to compare wasn't possible or needed from this environment, since the private key correctly lives only in the owner's password manager. Recorded here as the real reason a "Done" item's own mechanism can silently stop holding without a human re-checking it — not just a completeness gap in the original PR.

**101. Vertical Pod Autoscaler in recommendation mode**
- Purpose: #77 hand-trimmed CPU requests from observed usage and recovered ~1.4 real cores; VPA's recommender automates exactly that loop and produces a standing requests-vs-recommendation comparison — a natural pair with #65 (Kubecost) and its own article. Recommendation-only (`updateMode: "Off"`): on a single-node cluster an actuating VPA eviction is an outage.
- Acceptance Criteria: VPA deployed as an ArgoCD Application, `Off` mode for all workloads; a dashboard panel or scheduled report of recommended vs actual requests; one real adjustment cycle executed and recorded (adopt a recommendation, measure the delta against `kubectl describe node`).
- Dependencies: #92 (ksm/node metrics feed recommendation quality — confirm against VPA's real requirements, drop if unfounded).
- Priority: P2. Labels: `platform`, `observability`. **Sequence last among the hygiene items** (roadmap review, 2026-08-06) — #77 already did this project's own version of VPA's recommender loop by hand, once, on a one-node cluster with no churn to re-tune against; the marginal value of automating a loop that isn't currently being re-run is the lowest in this group.
  **Progress (2026-08-11, platform#163)**: deployed and live — `fairwinds-stable/vpa` 4.12.5, recommender only (`updater`/`admissionController` disabled: no actuation possible even if a `VerticalPodAutoscaler` object's own `updateMode` said otherwise, belt-and-suspenders beyond the AC's own `Off`-mode requirement). Real headroom checked live first (89% CPU requested at the time, ~440m free) before deciding to run only the one component recommendation-only actually needs. 50 real `VerticalPodAutoscaler` objects, one per real workload (every Deployment/StatefulSet/DaemonSet, plus the `api` Rollout — confirmed live Argo Rollouts' CRD implements the real `/scale` subresource VPA needs), all `updateMode: "Off"`, generated from the real live workload list rather than hand-typed against a 50-object transcription risk. Deep-reviewed given it's a new component on an already CPU-tight node — every label/port/target cross-checked against a real `helm template` render and live `kubectl get`, zero mismatches found across all 50 objects. Applied live: recommender `1/1 Running`, zero restarts anywhere, zero Hubble drops, real `RecommendationProvided: True` status confirmed on multiple VPA objects within minutes of going live (e.g. `api/api`'s own container already has a real, if still low-confidence, recommendation).
  **What's real and what isn't, stated honestly**: the AC's own "dashboard panel or scheduled report" needs a real data source, and one specific assumption turned out wrong, checked live rather than assumed — the recommender's own `/metrics` endpoint does **not** expose per-container recommended values as a scrapeable series (only internal quality histograms, `vpa_quality_cpu_recommendation_*`); the real recommendation values live only in each `VerticalPodAutoscaler` object's own `.status.recommendation` field. Checked whether `kube-state-metrics` (already deployed, #92) could bridge this natively — real, live `--help` output on the deployed image (`v2.19.1`) confirms `verticalpodautoscalers` isn't in its `--resources` list at all, ruling that path out cleanly rather than assuming it would work. Real candidates for closing this gap: kube-state-metrics' `customResourceState` config (a real, documented feature, adds RBAC + config complexity) or the AC's own offered alternative, a scheduled report reading `.status` directly (simpler, matches this project's own "no framework for a problem you don't have yet" norm) — not decided yet. **Also genuinely not done**: the recommendation data is minutes old at the time of this note, with the wide, low-confidence bounds that implies (`api/api`'s own real recommendation: target `15m` CPU, upper bound `20232m` — a real, honest illustration of why a "real adjustment cycle" against data this fresh would not be a meaningful demonstration). Both the report/panel and the real adjustment cycle wait for the data to actually mature, not for more engineering effort — same shape as #91/#94's own real, stated time-gates elsewhere in this backlog. Not marked Done.
  **Real adjustment cycle executed and recorded (2026-08-15, platform#180)**: the data matured (4 days old at the time of this check, real `.status.recommendation` no longer minutes-old placeholders). `aggregator`'s own real recommendation had settled to a meaningfully tighter number (target `15m` CPU, upperBound `28m`, vs. `api`'s own still-wide illustration above) — a genuine adjust candidate, cross-checked live against `kubectl top pod` (real measured usage: `5m`). CPU request lowered `250m` → `50m` (real headroom above both the 5m measured usage and VPA's own 28m upperBound, not a blind adopt of the raw 15m target); memory left untouched since VPA's own ~335Mi target already sat close to the existing 320Mi request. Deployed live: `kubectl describe node` confirmed a real, exact delta — `3395m/4000m (84%)` before → `3195m/4000m (79%)` after, freeing the predicted ~200m. New pod (`aggregator-6c45cc7bbb-*`) rolled with 0 restarts; a real, transient CPU burst was observed and tracked to confirm it wasn't sustained overuse of the new lower request — `kubectl top pod` read `948m` in the first ~10s post-restart (JIT warmup + Kafka Streams state-store restore, comfortably under the unchanged `1000m` limit, consistent with `services/aggregator/README.md`'s own `StateStoreRecoveryTest` numbers showing sub-second real restore time), settling to `254m` within ~40s and continuing to fall toward the real steady-state baseline. The scheduled-report/dashboard-panel half of this item's AC remains genuinely open — this closes only the "one real adjustment cycle" half.

**102. Grafana Beyla: eBPF auto-instrumentation A/B against the manual OTel stack**
- Purpose: a controlled experiment almost nobody can run outside a lab — zero-code eBPF RED metrics for services that already carry hand-built Micrometer/OTel instrumentation, on the same cluster, at the same time. "What do you actually lose with auto-instrumentation" is a genuinely novel comparison and this project owns both halves of it.
- Acceptance Criteria: Beyla deployed as an ArgoCD Application; its series for `api`/`workers`/`aggregator` plotted against the manual instrumentation's own on one dashboard; an honest write-up of the delta (cardinality, label fidelity, Kafka-consumer visibility, privilege requirements vs ADR 0028's unprivileged-injection precedent) — including if the answer is "manual wins".
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `observability`. **Done (2026-08-08, platform#131/#133/#134, ADR 0036)** — deployed unprivileged/capability-scoped (`privileged: false`; BPF/SYS_PTRACE/NET_RAW/CHECKPOINT_RESTORE/DAC_READ_SEARCH/PERFMON/SYS_ADMIN/NET_ADMIN, plus hostPID/hostNetwork the chart sets unconditionally — all confirmed via `helm template` before committing, not assumed). **A real live incident, not a config exercise**: the first deploy crash-looped on a real memcg OOM (`dmesg`'s own oom-killer log, real anon-rss+file-rss ~308Mi against a provisional 256Mi limit copied from otel-collector's own sizing) — a live `kubectl patch` bump was silently reverted by ArgoCD's own selfHeal before it could be validated, and a second live patch (`seccompProfile: Unconfined`, chased on a red herring — unrelated AppArmor ptrace-DENIED audit noise around the same crash window) never actually rendered through the chart's values schema in unprivileged mode at all (confirmed via `helm template`). Root cause only confirmed by reading the owner's own `dmesg` output directly; fixed by raising `resources.limits.memory` to 1Gi in git (platform#133) — the live patch had to be committed to actually stick. Verified stable for 65s+ with 0 restarts post-fix, `up{job="beyla"}` returns 1. **Dashboard + honest write-up (this item's own AC)**: `beyla-vs-manual` (platform#134) plots api/aggregator request rate Beyla vs manual side by side — real metric-name mismatch found live (Beyla's OTel-semconv `http_server_request_duration_seconds`, singular, vs Micrometer's `http_server_requests_seconds`, plural) — plus workers' Kafka message-processing p95 duration, Beyla-only, since workers has no HTTP API (ADR 0009/0011) for a manual instrument to exist for in the first place — the concrete "auto-instrumentation adds real new coverage" case, not a manufactured one. The privilege-cost side of the write-up lives in ADR 0036 itself, stated honestly against ADR 0028's fully-unprivileged precedent rather than a separate document.

**103. Grafana Faro: frontend observability for `visualizer` and `clinvar-viewer`**
- Purpose: both frontends are browser apps with zero telemetry — the only components whose real-user behavior is invisible. Web-vitals and error beacons close that, add a genuinely new signal class (RUM) to the lab, and would have caught #82's pre-merge ping race as user-visible behavior.
- Acceptance Criteria: a Faro collector (self-hosted or Grafana Cloud free tier — decision recorded) receiving web-vitals/errors from both static apps; a frontend dashboard; the privacy surface stated plainly (single-operator project — the data is the owner's/generator's own sessions, noted honestly rather than overclaimed as real-user monitoring at scale).
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `observability`, `backend`. **Done (2026-08-08, platform#132/#134, services#67, adamastorx#119, ADR 0037)** — self-hosted on the existing Alloy/Loki/Tempo stack (observability#3, ADR 0015), not a new component. **This decision was made twice**: the first pass (reverted before anything was pushed) wrongly concluded self-hosting needed a whole new Loki/Tempo/Alloy stack, resolved #103 as Won't-do, without checking this repo's real Application inventory first — corrected once the real state (all three already deployed) was checked; recorded honestly in ADR 0037 rather than silently fixed. `argocd/apps/alloy.yaml`'s River config gained a `faro.receiver`, config validated with the real `alloy validate`/`alloy fmt` binary before committing. Both frontends load `@grafana/faro-web-sdk` via the same CDN-script pattern their existing Chart.js/igv.js already use; `FARO_URL` follows the established config.js/typeof-guard deploy-time-config shape (backlog #82/#56). **Verified live, end to end, not just config-parsed**: a real synthetic event POSTed to Alloy's `faro.receiver` (`/collect`, confirmed from the component's own Go source, not guessed) returned 202, and the exact payload (`app_name`, `kind`, `message`) was confirmed queryable back out of the real running Loki via LogQL. `frontend-rum` dashboard (events-by-app, events-by-kind, raw log panel) built against those confirmed-real field names, not the SDK's own docs. Privacy stated plainly per this item's own AC: single-operator project, every session is the owner's own or `workload-generator`'s synthetic traffic, not real-user-at-scale monitoring.

---

## Cross-cutting

Items that don't belong to one milestone's feature scope — raised by an
independent staff-engineer-level review of the whole project (all four
repos, not one persona's remit) rather than by a specific epic's own
backlog. Same format, same discipline; no milestone gates these, they can
be picked up whenever.

**31. Top-level "what this project demonstrates" narrative doc**
- Purpose: The project's actual throughline — real incidents found and fixed live (namespace-scoped Secret/PVC sharing breaking twice, a Bitnami chart's auto-generated Secret silently regenerating, a double-ingestion race with no OOM evidence anywhere, four separate Spring Boot 4 autoconfiguration gotchas), a deliberate architecture pivot (ADR 0018 → ADR 0019) with the reasoning kept rather than erased, and what each milestone was actually chosen to prove — is currently only reconstructable by reading all ~20 ADRs plus `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` end to end. A hiring manager skimming the repo sees a feature list (README's Vision/Repository map) but not the connective narrative that is the actual portfolio value: the judgment calls, not just the tool list.
- Acceptance Criteria: One doc (e.g. `docs/WHY.md`) that names, in a few hundred words, the real bugs found and fixed, the ADR 0018→0019 pivot and why it happened, and the one-line "what this milestone proves" for each of M0-M5 — written for someone who will not read the ADRs, linking out to them for anyone who wants the detail. Linked prominently from the top-level README, not buried in `docs/`.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P0 (raised from P2, ADR 0031, 2026-08-06). Labels: `documentation`. **Done (2026-08-06, adamastorx#88)** — `docs/WHY.md`: the four recurring real-bug classes (namespace-scoped Secret/PVC sharing failing twice, a Bitnami chart's own idempotency logic regenerating a live credential, a concurrency bug found by trusting missing OOM evidence over the plausible story, four Spring Boot 4 autoconfiguration traps), the ADR 0018→0019 pivot and why, and one line per milestone M0-M13 (the stale M0-M5 scope above corrected in the build, not copied forward). Linked from the README's first screen. **A real, additional drift instance found and fixed in the same PR**: README's own "Scope boundaries" still listed Cilium and service mesh as excluded tools; `.claude/PROJECT.md` already correctly recorded both exclusions as overturned (ADR 0023/0024) — the internal doc had the correction, the public-facing one didn't, until this PR.

**32. Keep `.claude/PROJECT.md`'s "Current milestone" section refreshed as a routine step, not an afterthought**
- Purpose: `PROJECT.md` calls itself "the stable picture" other repos point back to instead of duplicating, and `.claude/WORKFLOW.md`'s "Post-merge sweep" already names `.claude/PROJECT.md`'s current-state sections as something the `documentation-engineer` agent checks after any merge with architectural/operational impact — but in practice it wasn't: this section was last updated at services#4 and still read "Current milestone: M2 ... services#5 remaining" after Redis (services#5), all of M3, and all of M5 had shipped and `docs/architecture/overview.md` had been refreshed many times over in the same window. The documented process exists on paper; nothing catches it silently not running. Fixed directly as part of this review's own PR — this item is about making it stick, not the one-time correction.
- Acceptance Criteria: The post-merge sweep step in `.claude/WORKFLOW.md` gets a concrete, checkable trigger (e.g. any PR that closes a backlog item or completes a milestone must touch `PROJECT.md`'s "Current milestone" section in the same PR, checked in the `CONTRIBUTING.md` PR checklist, not left to a separate sweep that can be silently skipped). A second full pass confirms no other "canonical" doc (this file, `docs/architecture/overview.md`, README) has silently drifted the same way right now.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `documentation`. **Superseded by automation (2026-08-07, adamastorx#102)** — this item's own trigger (a PR-checklist reminder) is exactly the mechanism #83 proved, twice, doesn't stick on its own. #97's CI roster check (`docs/roadmap/backlog.md` §97) replaces the checklist with a check that fails the build instead of relying on anyone remembering to look. Not re-marked Done a second time; closed here as the honest record of why the checklist approach itself is retired, not just the one drift it caught.

**83. `docs/architecture/overview.md` is stale again — the exact drift #32 already named, recurring**
- Purpose: found live during a staff-engineer review, 2026-08-02. The file's "Live today" section still describes the shape as of M5 — no `watchlist-service` (#53), no async job control plane (#54), no per-tenant API keys/rate limiting at the edge (#56), no Argo Rollouts canary (#46), no KEDA autoscaling (#63), no Pyroscope/continuous profiling (#57), no chaos-scenario findings, no M13. This is real evidence that #32's own fix (a checkable trigger in the PR checklist) has not actually stuck — the same drift it was written to catch happened again, on the same file it explicitly named as one to watch. Recorded honestly rather than silently re-fixed and left to recur a third time.
- Acceptance Criteria: `docs/architecture/overview.md` rewritten to describe the real, current, live shape of the system — every component listed in this document's own audit is current: `api` as an Argo Rollouts `Rollout` (not a plain Deployment), `workers` autoscaled by KEDA, the API-key/rate-limit middleware chain on the public Ingress, `watchlist-service`'s outbox-plus-relay pattern, `clinvar-service`'s async job control plane, Pyroscope as the fourth observability pillar, and M13's existence (even while gated on M7, so a reader isn't surprised by a milestone with no running components yet). Separately and just as important: #32's own trigger mechanism is checked for real — confirm whether the PR-checklist trigger it describes actually exists in `CONTRIBUTING.md` today, and if it doesn't (or exists but isn't being followed), that gap is named explicitly here rather than assumed fixed because #32 says "Done" elsewhere.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P1. Labels: `documentation`. **Recurred a third time, 2026-08-06** (found by the staff-engineer review, `docs/reviews/`): overview.md again claimed M13's five services "do not exist yet" two days after this same repo's backlog declared M13 Done — the exact drift this item's AC names, on the exact file it names, after its fix had shipped. Fixed again in the ADR 0031 PR. **Superseded by automation (2026-08-07, adamastorx#102)** — #97's CI roster check now fails the build if any live custom-service component ever goes unmentioned in overview.md again; verified the real, current overview.md passes clean today. Not re-marked Done a fourth time on the same file — closed here as superseded, the honest record that a human-checklist fix for this specific file failed three times before automation replaced it.

**84. Kafka CLI tooling is a real operational risk on this single-broker, tightly-constrained pod**
- Purpose: found live, twice independently, on 2026-08-02 while bringing up M13's first two services. (a) The #78 (`market-data-ingestor`) agent's first `kafka-topics.sh --list` exec into `kafka-controller-0` triggered a real `OOMKilled` (confirmed via `kubectl describe pod`) — the broker self-recovered within ~90s, but this is the same fragility class #75/#77 already documented (a single-broker dev cluster with narrow memory headroom, `KAFKA_HEAP_OPTS` sized to 75% of a tight limit), now confirmed to be triggerable by routine CLI tooling, not just backlog/OOM scenarios. A stray `kafka-console-consumer.sh` process from that same session was also left running, idle and memory-stable but un-killable via `kubectl exec` (`Permission denied`, the same PID-signaling restriction #73 already found for `pg_ctl reload`/`kill -HUP 1`) — needs a human to clean up during a routine pod restart. (b) Separately, `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`'s "ArgoCD stuck-operation gremlin" section gained a second confirmed trigger the same day: patching an Application's `.operation.sync.revision` to `"HEAD"` on a Helm-chart source fails manifest generation but the retry silently re-applies a stale cached manifest and reports a misleading per-resource "Succeeded" under an overall `Error` phase — real-live-hit while syncing `kafka` to pick up backlog #79's new topic, see that section for the full account and the fix (never set `revision` in a manual sync patch).
- Acceptance Criteria: a real decision recorded, not just the incident notes above — candidates include a documented "never exec `kafka-topics.sh --list` directly against the broker pod, use `--describe --topic <name>` or the chart's own provisioning Job instead" operating rule, a memory headroom bump for `kafka-controller-0` if a real measurement justifies it (same discipline #75 used, not a guess), or accepting the risk explicitly with the documented workaround (SESSION_STATE.md's ArgoCD-sync-based topic creation path, now proven, over raw `kubectl exec`). Whichever is chosen, verified against a real repro (the same `kafka-topics.sh --list` exec) showing the fix holds, or the acceptance stated plainly if the decision is "accept and document" rather than "fix."
- Dependencies: #75, #77 (same resource-constraint root cause).
- Priority: P2. Labels: `platform`, `observability`. **Done (2026-08-11, platform#164)** — memory headroom bump chosen (the AC's own second candidate), justified by a real measurement first, not a guess: `kubectl top pod` showed `kafka-controller-0` at ~1427Mi/1536Mi (93%) steady-state, broker alone, no client exec running — the real reason a second JVM (the CLI client, spawned inside the same container/cgroup) tips it over. Node memory headroom checked live first too (43% requested at the time) before picking a real number, same discipline #75 used. `controller.resources` bumped `512Mi/1536Mi` → `1024Mi/2560Mi` (request/limit); CPU left untouched, since it's the actually-scarce resource here and this fix doesn't need to touch it. **Verified against the real repro this item's own AC asked for, not just deployed and assumed**: synced live, `kafka-controller-0` restarted cleanly (`1/1 Ready`, zero restarts), the chart's own provisioning Job re-ran idempotently and completed, then the exact real command that OOMKilled the broker on 2026-08-02 (`kubectl exec ... kafka-topics.sh --bootstrap-server localhost:9092 --list`) was re-run twice — both times exit `0`, the real topic list returned (9 topics, including the newer M13 ones), zero restarts either time. Honest post-fix note: real memory usage right after the restart was much lower (~689Mi) than the pre-fix 1427Mi steady-state — expected (a freshly-started JVM hasn't re-accumulated page cache/heap pressure yet), not itself proof of anything; the real headroom that matters is the ceiling, which grew from 109Mi above the old steady-state to 1133Mi above it, comfortably covering a CLI client's real footprint even once usage climbs back to where it was.

**85. `aggregator`'s Kafka Streams client crashed silently on first real sync — RocksDB/Alpine incompatibility, and a liveness gap that let it go unnoticed**
- Purpose: found live during backlog #81's first real cluster sync, 2026-08-03. rocksdbjni's bundled native library needs glibc/libstdc++; this project's shared Java runtime image (`services/Dockerfile`) is `eclipse-temurin:25-jre-alpine`, whose musl-libc base doesn't ship `libstdc++` by default. The real pod started, Spring/actuator came up fully healthy, and ArgoCD/Kubernetes both reported `Healthy`/`Running` throughout — but the Kafka Streams client itself hit a real `UnsatisfiedLinkError` ("Error loading shared library libstdc++.so.6") the moment it tried to open a real RocksDB-backed state store against the real, reachable broker, then shut itself down (`SHUTDOWN_CLIENT`). **A second, independent real gap made this a silent failure rather than a visible one**: `aggregator`'s readiness/liveness probes (Spring Boot's default health groups) don't gate on Kafka Streams' own state — a deliberate choice recorded in `platform/kubernetes/aggregator/deployment.yaml`'s own comment, reasoned against the *restore* window (avoiding probe flapping during a real ~45s restart), but never considered against a *permanent* crash. The REST API's own `isReady()` check correctly kept returning `503` (an honest signal), but nothing told Kubernetes to actually restart the pod — a fully-dead-but-"Healthy" pod could have sat that way indefinitely. Neither the CI Docker smoke test (an unreachable-broker scenario, which never reaches real RocksDB store-opening) nor local `mvn verify` (runs on the host JVM, not this Alpine image) could have caught this — only a real cluster sync did.
- Acceptance Criteria: two real fixes, not one glossing over the other. (a) The RocksDB/Alpine root cause — `services#52` added `libstdc++`/`libgcc` to the shared runtime image; verify live (redeploy `aggregator`, confirm the Kafka Streams client reaches and stays `RUNNING`, confirm `GET /aggregates` returns real data, not `503`). (b) The liveness gap — a real decision on whether `aggregator`'s liveness probe should gate on Kafka Streams reaching/staying `RUNNING` (e.g. wiring `KafkaStreams.State` into a custom `HealthIndicator`, distinct from readiness so the *restore-window* reasoning that led to the current design is preserved for readiness while liveness catches a genuine, permanent crash) — stated and either implemented or explicitly deferred with the real tradeoff recorded, not left as an implicit gap a second time.
- Dependencies: #81.
- Priority: P1. Labels: `platform`, `observability`, `bug`. **Done (2026-08-03/2026-08-05, services#52/#53/#57, platform#98/#99/#105)** — (a) fixed, verified live, twice over. `services#52` added `libstdc++`/`libgcc`, resolving the RocksDB/Alpine `UnsatisfiedLinkError`; redeploying with that alone surfaced a **second, independent real bug** in the exact same silent-but-Healthy shape: `BoundedRocksDbConfigSetter` constructed a fresh `BlockBasedTableConfig` instead of reusing the instance Kafka Streams itself attaches to `Options`, breaking its internal RocksDB metrics-recorder wiring the moment a store was actually opened against real traffic (`org.apache.kafka.streams.errors.ProcessorStateException`, again `SHUTDOWN_CLIENT`, again a `Healthy`-looking pod). `services#53` fixed that too (retrieve-and-mutate `options.tableFormatConfig()`, exactly what the real exception message names as the fix). Redeployed a third time: real logs confirmed `RUNNING` sustained for 60+ real seconds with no further exception, `GET /aggregates` correctly returned `200 []`/`404` (real "no data yet" responses, not `503`) — the app is genuinely healthy now, not just Kubernetes-reported healthy. (b) **now closed (services#57)**: new `KafkaStreamsLivenessHealthIndicator` fails `/actuator/health/liveness` only on the real, terminal `KafkaStreams.State.ERROR` — the exact state both real (a) incidents hit while the pod stayed `Healthy` — wired into the `liveness` health group only (`management.endpoint.health.group.liveness.include: [livenessState, kafkaStreams]`); readiness stays deliberately ungated (the real ~45s restore/rejoin window every restart goes through must not flap the pod out of the Service's endpoints, `AggregateQueryService`'s own `503` already gives a more honest signal). Verified with a real, live-broker-induced crash: a dedicated Kafka Streams instance forced into genuine `ERROR` via a deliberately-throwing topology, a real Spring context boot proving the health-group wiring end to end. Real deploy caught a genuine cross-PR compile break before merge: this branch (opened before services#56) constructed `AggregatorProperties` with its old 7-arg shape; #56's `latestPriceStoreName`/`latestSentimentStoreName` fields had already landed on `main`, so the branch no longer compiled — fixed by merging `main` in and updating the constructor call to match, confirmed green via real CI (`build: pass`), not assumed. Deployed live (platform#105): fresh headroom check (83% CPU requested, unchanged — a same-request redeploy, not new capacity), new pod reached `1/1 Running` in 55s (within the ~45s restore window plus probe margin, no flapping), `/actuator/health/liveness` real `200 {"status":"UP"}`, `GET /aggregates` still serving real live data post-deploy.

**86. `market-data-ingestor`'s Kafka producer permanently poisoned by a `NoClassDefFoundError`, and the error log that hid it for two days**
- Purpose: found live, 2026-08-05, while checking why `visualizer`/`aggregator` showed no data. `market-data-ingestor`'s real pod (`f955f9484-j4btt`, 0 restarts, up since 2026-08-02) had been silently broken since **2026-08-03T08:00:03Z** — a real `java.lang.ExceptionInInitializerError`, immediately followed on the next reconnect by `java.lang.NoClassDefFoundError: Could not initialize class org.apache.kafka.clients.admin.AdminClientConfig`, repeating on every Finnhub reconnect for over two days straight (a real, permanent JVM class-poisoning: once a class's static initializer throws, every later reference to that class throws `NoClassDefFoundError` for the rest of that JVM's life, per the JVM spec — no self-heal possible short of a restart). The pod stayed `1/1 Running`/`Healthy` the entire time (liveness/readiness don't check this), so nothing surfaced it — the same silent-but-Healthy shape #85 already found twice in `aggregator`. **A second, compounding real bug made the first one nearly undiagnosable**: `FinnhubWebSocketClient`'s `onError` handler logs only `error.toString()` (confirmed by reading the actual log output — a bare one-line message, no stack trace, no `Caused by` chain anywhere in over two days of repeated occurrences), so the real root cause of the original `ExceptionInInitializerError` — why `AdminClientConfig`'s static init actually failed the first time — is unrecoverable after the fact. A `kubectl delete pod` restart (confirmed safe, reversible, no state to lose per this service's own design) cleared the poisoned class state; the new pod ran clean for the first 15+ seconds post-restart with no recurrence, but whether the *original* trigger can recur is unknown without the missing stack trace.
- Acceptance Criteria: (a) Fix the logging gap first — `FinnhubWebSocketClient`'s error handler (and any other bare `.toString()`-only exception logging in this module) must log the real `Throwable` object (SLF4J's own `log.warn("...", ex)` overload, which prints the full stack trace and cause chain), not just its string form. This alone would have made this item's own root-cause investigation possible; without it, any recurrence is equally undiagnosable. (b) With real stack traces available, if this recurs, investigate the actual root cause (a classpath conflict, a resource-loading race during concurrent static init, or something else) and fix or explicitly document it — don't just restart-and-move-on a second time. (c) Consider whether this is another real case for #85(b)'s same liveness-probe-hardening question — a permanently poisoned Kafka producer is exactly the kind of "Healthy pod, dead functionality" state a liveness probe checking real producer/websocket health (not just Tomcat being up) would catch.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P1. Labels: `platform`, `observability`, `bug`. **(a) Done (2026-08-05, services#58, platform#106)** — fixed and deployed live, four sites grepped and fixed, not just `onError`; full details in the prior note below. **(b) and (c) now done too — a real recurrence, found live 2026-08-07/08 while chasing backlog #91's own live-verification blocker**, gave (a)'s logging fix its first real test and it worked: the pod's `market_data_ticks_published_total` stayed at `0` for over 10 hours despite the websocket genuinely reconnecting (`market_data_websocket_reconnects_total` climbing), and this time the real root cause was captured, not lost. **Root cause (b), finally real, not theorized**: `KafkaTemplate`'s own observation/tracing code (`KafkaTemplate.observeSend` → `clusterId()`) touches `org.apache.kafka.clients.admin.AdminClientConfig`'s static initializer, which threw a real `ConfigException` (`Invalid value org.apache.kafka.common.security.oauthbearer.DefaultJwtRetriever for configuration sasl.oauthbearer.jwt.retriever.class: Class ... could not be found`) from an unrelated `HttpClient-1-Worker-0` thread — the exact JVM class-poisoning mechanism this item's Purpose already named, now with the trigger actually on record. Not fixed at the root (a JDK/kafka-clients class-loading interaction under concurrent access, not a bug in this project's own code, and not reliably reproducible without live traffic to test against) — **fixed at the boundary instead, matching (c)'s own suggestion**: `KafkaProducerLivenessHealthIndicator` (new) trips liveness `DOWN`, permanently, the moment any publish attempt's cause chain contains a real `LinkageError` — precise enough to never misfire on an ordinary, recoverable Kafka exception (confirmed via a real, targeted unit test using the actual `NoClassDefFoundError`/`TimeoutException` types, not mocks), same precision `KafkaStreamsLivenessHealthIndicator` already established for `aggregator`'s own Kafka Streams client. A second, real, independently-found bug fixed in the same pass: `StockPriceTickPublisher.publish()` only wrapped the *async* completion in error handling — the real stack trace showed `kafkaTemplate.send()` itself throwing the `LinkageError` *synchronously*, before a future was ever returned, propagating uncaught into the websocket message handler on every subsequent real trade. Now wrapped in a `try`/`catch` around the whole call, not just `.whenComplete()`. **Live redeploy confirmed clean (2026-08-08, platform#126)**: fresh pod (`kubectl delete pod` implicitly via the image bump, no manual step needed), real `Cluster ID` resolved successfully in the startup logs (the exact code path that threw before), zero `LinkageError`/`NoClassDefFoundError` since, `market_data_ticks_published_total` climbed to `5` within the first minute of real traffic (the fallback path — see #91's own note, market was closed at verification time) — the producer works end to end now, not just "doesn't crash."

**87. Always-fresh watchlist data: `market-data-ingestor` REST-poll fallback + `aggregator` latest-known-state**
- Purpose: real, live gap found 2026-08-05 checking `visualizer` after the #86 restart — `GET /aggregates` went back to `[]` once the 15-minute tumbling window that had real data in it rolled over with no new tick (proven live: a manually-injected window populated the endpoint, then a second check ~8 hours later found it empty again, the concrete trigger for this item). Owner's explicit ask: keep stock prices reasonably fresh without hammering Finnhub, keep news/sentiment polling as-is (already cheap, RSS-only — owner explicitly overrode a proposed poll-interval change here, keep it at 5 minutes), and make sure `visualizer` always shows *something* current when opened, not an empty page between windows or outside market hours.
- Acceptance Criteria: (part B) `market-data-ingestor` gains an independent scheduled REST-poll fallback against Finnhub's `/quote` endpoint for all watchlisted tickers, publishing through the same `stock.price.tick` topic/wire-shape as the existing websocket path — a supplement for off-hours/gap coverage, not a replacement, and deliberately not feeding `StaleFeedMetrics` (would mask a real websocket outage during real market hours). Real rate-limit accounting stated, not assumed. (part C) `aggregator` resolves price and sentiment independently, preferring the current window when it has data and falling back to a non-windowed "latest known" value otherwise, with an honest `priceAsOf`/`sentimentAsOf` timestamp on every response so staleness is visible, never fabricated as a live movement. `404` narrows to mean "never seen a tick for this ticker at all," not "nothing in this window."
- Dependencies: #78, #81.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `backend`, `architecture`. **Done (2026-08-05, services#55/#56, platform#103/#104)** — part B (`FinnhubQuotePoller`, `@Scheduled` every 30 minutes, user-confirmed cadence): 5 calls/cycle, 10/hour average, trivially within Finnhub's free-tier 60 calls/minute; `volume` published as `BigDecimal.ZERO` (stated v1 gap, `StockPriceTick.volume()` is non-null everywhere else); catch block logs only the exception's simple class name, never the message/`toString()`, since the request URI carries the real API key as a query param. Deployed live: `market_data_quote_poll_succeeded_total=5.0`/`market_data_quote_ticks_published_total=5.0`, zero failures. Part C: two new non-windowed `KTable`s (`latest-price-store`/`latest-sentiment-store`, via `KStream.toTable()` on the already-ticker-keyed sources, confirmed via `Topology#describe()` to add no repartition step) alongside the existing windowed stores, resolved independently per price/sentiment through `AggregateQueryService.latestKnownState(...)`; `priceAsOf`/`sentimentAsOf` sourced from Kafka Streams' own `Timestamped*Store` types (KIP-258), not a second hand-rolled timestamp. Bounded by key-space (5 tickers), not time — doesn't reopen ADR 0011's resolution for the windowed stores. **Live, verified for real**: fresh `kubectl describe node` headroom check before sync (83% CPU requested, ~655m free — a redeploy of an already-running pod at the same request, not new capacity), new pod reached `RUNNING` with all tasks restored and zero errors in a clean 15s log window (a burst of transient `UnknownTopicOrPartitionException` during the new changelog topics' own creation, self-resolved, not a recurrence of #85's silent-crash shape), real `GET /aggregates` returned all 5 tickers with real, current `priceAsOf` timestamps. Follow-up not yet built: `visualizer` doesn't read/display the new `priceAsOf`/`sentimentAsOf` fields yet (flagged by the #56 PR itself) — a natural next step to actually surface "as of N minutes ago" in the UI, not yet requested as its own item.

**104. M7 gate decision: dedicated host vs interim VM-based multi-node**
- Purpose: M7 and M12 are hostage to one physical hardware move with no date attached, and "blocked indefinitely" is how labs stall. The review (B3) names an interim that doesn't betray the owned-hardware story: two k3s agent VMs (multipass/lima) on the existing machine, provisioned through the same Terraform remote-exec path #5 already proved — unblocking #48–#52 and the Cilium/Istio learning immediately, at the honest cost of no real power-loss test (#52's hard-power-off AC stays hardware-only, stated as a gap).
- Acceptance Criteria: a recorded decision — dedicated host by a stated date, the VM interim, or explicitly accept the gate with a review date — as an ADR addendum or this item's closing note. "Drift" is the one answer not allowed. If the VM interim is chosen: #48's AC adapted accordingly and the old-single-node-path-stays-supported constraint (a Terraform variable, not a fork) preserved.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P1. Labels: `platform`. **Done (2026-08-08, ADR 0035)** — the VM interim chosen, for real, not drift: 2 k3s agent VMs (multipass/lima) on the existing laptop, provisioned through the same Terraform path #5 already proved. Dedicated-host-by-a-date rejected for lack of an honest date to state; accept-the-gate-with-a-review-date rejected as leaving real multi-node learning blocked when a cheap, honest interim exists. #48's own AC updated to reflect VM agents, single-node path preserved as a Terraform variable. Building the actual VMs is #48's own scope, not this item's — this item closes with the decision recorded, matching its own AC.

**105. HikariCP acquisition-timeout decision (companion to #43)**
- Purpose: chaos scenario 2 found the Postgres-side twin of #43's Kafka finding — a real ~30s HikariCP connection-acquisition hang before a 500, user-facing — and `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` records plainly that "the Postgres side doesn't have its own item yet." Two documented, user-facing hangs, both currently undecided.
- Acceptance Criteria: a documented decision (ADR addendum or backlog note) for the HikariCP side, explicitly linked with #43, both stating whether #60 (Istio timeouts/circuit-breaking, M7) is their intended dataplane answer — if so, these items' documented hangs become #60's before/after evidence rather than independent fixes.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `backend`, `observability`. **Done (2026-08-08, ADR 0024 addendum)** — the decision was already made in ADR 0024's own Context section (it cites both real hangs as its own motivating evidence), just never explicitly cross-referenced from this item's own number. Addendum added, closing the loop: yes, ADR 0024/#60 is the intended dataplane answer for both #43 and this item; neither gets an independent per-client fix, both stay open (gated on M7/#104) as #60's real before/after evidence. A real, unrelated bug found and fixed in the same pass, drive-by: ADR 0024's own file ended with two stray literal lines (`</content>`/`</invoke>`), a real leaked-tool-call artifact from some earlier session, not part of the ADR's actual content — stripped. **Un-deferred (2026-08-09, ADR 0040)** — the new ADR 0040 reverses ADR 0024's 2026-08-08 addendum: with #59/#60 superseded, this item proceeds as an app-level fix, no longer gated on M7 — HikariCP's `connectionTimeout` tuned with a real fail-fast path, plus a Resilience4j circuit breaker on `api`'s outbound `clinvar-service` call (the traffic-control piece #60 would have provided at the dataplane). Java/`api` only for now; the Python side gets no independent fix since no Python-side hang is actually documented. Proven the way #60 would have been: chaos scenario 2 re-run, before/after latency recorded as a dated postscript in the existing fact pack. **Implemented for real (2026-08-15, services#74)** — the un-deferred decision above had never actually been coded (real check: no `hikari`/`resilience4j` config anywhere in `api`'s `application.yml`/`pom.xml`). `spring.datasource.hikari.connection-timeout` tuned to 3000ms (down from Boot's 30s default, the exact real hang chaos scenario 2 found). The same class of gap also existed, undocumented, on `api`'s outbound `clinvar-service` HTTP client (`ClinVarServiceClient`'s `RestClient.builder()` had no connect/read timeout at all) — fixed with the same explicit-timeout pattern already established (and found-on-review) in `watchlist-service`'s `NtfyClient`, 2s connect/3s read. A new test proves it live: a fake upstream that deliberately never responds, real wall-clock elapsed time asserted well under the fake server's own 10s delay, proving a real timeout fired rather than the request completing or the test itself hanging. **Resilience4j circuit breaker deliberately not added in this pass** — the two explicit timeouts above already close the concrete, documented hang; a circuit breaker is a further resilience improvement (short-circuiting repeated failures instead of paying the timeout on every request during a sustained outage) worth its own real load-based verification, not bundled into this fix. Real remaining scope if picked up later.

**106. Give `workers` a real side effect, or restate it honestly as test infrastructure**
- Purpose: `workers` consumes `work-items` and logs — a log-only consumer that has quietly become load-bearing test infrastructure (KEDA's scaling target in #63, the lag alert's subject in #21/#76). The review (B5) flags that a skeptical reader will notice the project's flagship consumer does nothing; either answer fixes that, the current implicit state doesn't.
- Acceptance Criteria: either a real side effect (a persisted processed result or a real transformation, with its own metric) or an honest restatement in `workers`' README and ADR 0011's consequences that it is deliberate test/load infrastructure by design.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `backend`. **Done (2026-08-08, services#65, ADR 0011 addendum)** — honest-restatement path: `workers`' README now states plainly that the log-only handler is deliberate test infrastructure (KEDA's own scaling target, #21/#76's lag-alert subject), not an unfinished feature. Companion ADR 0011 addendum records the same in its own consequences section.

**107. The ntfy alert topic's only stated protection is defeated by the repo that states it**
- Purpose: found 2026-08-06 reviewing the ADR 0031 PR, not by the review itself. `platform/argocd/apps/prometheus.yaml`'s Alertmanager receiver posts to `https://ntfy.sh/adamastorx-alerts-<topic>`, and the comment immediately above it states the threat model plainly and correctly: *"ntfy topics are public-by-topic-name with no auth, so the only protection is not being guessable."* The topic name is then committed, in full, in that same file — in a **public** repository (`AdamastorX/platform`, confirmed `visibility: public`). The single stated protection is therefore not merely weak, it is fully defeated, and has been since the receiver shipped. Real, current impact: anyone can subscribe to the project's live alert stream (alert names, summaries, hostnames, and whatever future alerts add — a real information-disclosure channel into the cluster's internals), and anyone can publish arbitrary messages to the same topic (alert-fatigue nuisance, and a plausible social-engineering path given the owner treats these as real pages). This is not a leaked credential — ntfy topics carry no secret — which is exactly why it slipped past secret-scanning and past a careful human review that read this same file: it is a *design* whose stated assumption the repository itself invalidates. **Related and worth deciding together, not after:** #88 exposes Grafana publicly and read-only; its AC should pin down explicitly whether the anonymous Viewer role can reach Explore, since arbitrary PromQL against the Prometheus datasource would expose #56's per-tenant API-key labels and internal hostnames — verified live against the real deployed Grafana, not assumed from a documented default.
- Acceptance Criteria: the ntfy topic rotated to a fresh random value and moved out of git into the existing out-of-band Secret pattern (`bootstrap/create-stateful-secrets.sh` + a `secretKeyRef`/Alertmanager secret file, same shape every other real credential here uses), with the old topic treated as burned and never reused. The file's own comment updated so the stated threat model matches reality rather than contradicting it. Verified live: a real alert still reaches the owner's device after rotation (the whole point of ADR 0020's notification path — a rotation that silently breaks alerting is worse than the exposure). Separately, a check of whether any *other* "protection is that it isn't guessable" value is committed anywhere across the four repos — the class, not just this instance.
- Dependencies: none. Should land before #88 (public exposure widens the blast radius of anything in this class).
- Priority: P1. Labels: `platform`, `security`. **Done (2026-08-06, platform#107)** — topic rotated (fresh 16-byte hex value) and moved to a `ntfy-webhook-url` Secret (`prometheus` namespace) provisioned via the existing out-of-band pattern, read through Alertmanager's own `url_file` config key (real upstream feature, mutually exclusive with `url`) mounted via `alertmanager.extraSecretMounts`. Hit the same child-Application-lag gremlin this session already documented (`docs/SESSION_STATE.md`): the first sync left the StatefulSet's pod spec completely unchanged (11-day-old pod, 0 restarts, no new volume) because the `prometheus` Application's own `spec.source.helm.valuesObject` hadn't yet picked up the new `extraSecretMounts` key from git — fixed with `argocd.argoproj.io/refresh=hard` on `root`, confirmed the value appeared, then re-synced. **Verified live, end to end, not just config-parsed**: a real synthetic alert POSTed to Alertmanager's own `/api/v2/alerts` was matched to the `ntfy` receiver, and polling the new topic's own `ntfy.sh/<topic>/json` history back confirmed the exact alert payload was actually delivered through `url_file` — then resolved cleanly so nothing was left firing. GitHub Actions was mid-outage (a real, confirmed platform-wide incident, not local) when this merged, so `platform`'s required CI checks (including `helm-render`) could not run; merged with `--admin` after explicit confirmation, with the live pod verification above standing in for what `helm-render` would have caught (a malformed `extraSecretMounts` value would have failed the same way this session's own gremlin did, visibly and revertibly). Old topic left in place but unused — treated as burned, not deleted (ntfy has no deletion API; a dead topic with no subscriber is inert). **Cross-repo sweep done per this item's own AC**: grepped all four repos for ntfy/webhook/api-key/token patterns; the only two other `ntfy.sh/` references found (`docs/adr/0020-slos-and-alerting.md`, `observability/chaos/02-postgresql-unavailable.md`) both already used a placeholder/redacted form, not a real committed topic — no other instance of this class found.

**108. Mimir experiment: multi-tenant/remote-write long-term storage, once retention alone stops being enough**
- Purpose: split from #94 during roadmap review (2026-08-06) — #94's own real numbers (2Gi Prometheus PVC, 176GB free on the node) show retention length was the actual blocker for the SLO-over-time report, not query fan-out or multi-tenancy, so #94 no longer needs Mimir as a prerequisite. The Mimir experiment itself is still real, stated lab value (#18a's original scoping: "run it whenever there's an actual question it answers") — this item is that question, kept honest by not being load-bearing for anything else.
- Acceptance Criteria: Mimir deployed as an ArgoCD Application (monolithic mode, real PVC, resource cost measured against #77's headroom accounting, on top of whatever #94 has already consumed); Prometheus remote-writes to it; Grafana gains it as a second datasource alongside (not replacing) Prometheus. #18a's write-up completed honestly: what it adds at this project's real single-node scale over plain retention, what it costs to run, and whether the answer is "not yet worth it" — that is a legitimate, stated outcome, not a foregone conclusion. Rollback path stated (removing remote-write leaves Prometheus/#94's retention standing on its own).
- Dependencies: #94.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `observability`, `platform`. **Done (2026-08-08, platform#135/#136/#137, adamastorx#121, ADR 0038)** — real Mimir 3.1.2 in its own documented monolithic mode (`-target=all,alertmanager`), hand-written manifests under `kubernetes/mimir/` rather than the `mimir-distributed` chart, which has no single-process deployment mode at all (checked live: built entirely around 10+ separate distributed components at every preset, the exact machinery ADR 0011/0014/0032 already reject). Config proven with the real binary locally before every commit, not assumed. **Three real bugs found live, none assumed away**: (1) manual sync failed outright with "namespaces mimir not found" — `CreateNamespace=true` wasn't honored by a raw `kubectl patch`-triggered sync operation, fixed by creating the namespace directly; (2) the real pod passed its own readiness probe throughout while silently rejecting every real remote_write with a 500 ("at least 2 live replicas required, could only find 1") — `ingester.ring.replication_factor` defaults to 3 even in monolithic mode, set to 1, the only value a single replica can ever satisfy; (3) a second real limit then blocked most writes — the default 150,000-per-tenant series cap against this cluster's real, measured `prometheus_tsdb_head_series` of ~186,604, raised to 300,000 with real headroom. A fourth, smaller real gap found and deliberately left as a stated limitation, not silently patched: otel-collector's own self-monitoring series carry a label literally named `server.address` (valid for Prometheus's own local TSDB, rejected by Mimir's stricter remote-write validation) — real, concrete evidence for the write-up that Mimir's ingest path enforces rules plain Prometheus doesn't. **Verified live, end to end, not just deployed**: `up{job=...}` for `api`/`beyla`/`workers`/etc. queried back out through Mimir's own `/prometheus`-prefixed API returns real, current data; Grafana's `Mimir` datasource health check returns `status: OK`. **The honest write-up (this item's own AC)**: not yet worth it as a standing piece of this cluster's real architecture — real CPU headroom was already tight before this (91% of allocatable requested) and the `filesystem` backend Mimir's own startup log calls "for development and testing only" delivers no real durability this single node's own disk didn't already have. Kept deployed as the real, completed experiment rather than torn down to match its own conclusion — full reasoning in ADR 0038.

**109. AC template requires observability (dashboard, SLO row, alert, runbook) for any new service, checked by #97**
- Purpose: ADR 0031's own root-cause finding for the M13 gap (#90's Purpose, and the ADR itself): #78–#82's ACs asked for real metrics and got them — nothing shipped in violation of its own AC. What was missing is that no AC template asked for the dashboard/SLO-row/alert/runbook standard ADR 0017/0020 set for every service before M13. #90 fixes M13's instance of the gap; without a template change, the next new service repeats it, and #97's CI check would then be permanently correcting after the fact instead of preventing.
- Acceptance Criteria: the project's own service-AC template/checklist (wherever new-service ACs are drafted — `.claude/agents/` persona guidance and/or `CONTRIBUTING.md`, whichever actually governs this in practice, checked live rather than assumed) gains a standing line: a new service's AC is not complete without naming its golden-signal dashboard, its ADR 0020 SLO-table row, and any consumer-lag/staleness alert with a runbook, or an explicit stated reason one doesn't apply. #97's roster check (part a) extended to flag a newly-synced ArgoCD Application with no corresponding dashboard/SLO-table entry after a stated grace period, closing the loop instead of relying on the template alone.
- Dependencies: #90, #97.
- Priority: P1. Labels: `documentation`, `observability`. **Done (2026-08-08, adamastorx#115, platform#129)** — `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s PR checklist gained the standing line. `check-roster-drift.sh` (#97a) extended with the grace-period check: a live component past a stated grace period (default 14d, via `git log --follow`'s real first-commit timestamp on its own `argocd/apps/<name>.yaml`, requiring `fetch-depth: 0`) must be mentioned in `grafana.yaml` and ADR 0020's SLO table, or state a reason in the same files — the exact same "mentioned anywhere" grep as the real-entry case, so no separate exemption allowlist to let drift. Proven with a synthetic, self-contained git-repo CI fixture (`scripts/fixtures/roster-drift-grace/`) exercising both the failure case and the pass-with-reason case, not just tested against real repo state. Real, live exemptions applied to the three components that legitimately have none: `clinvar-viewer`/`visualizer` (static frontends, no backend signal of their own) and `workload-generator` (synthetic traffic infra, not a service) — found by running the real check against real `grafana.yaml`/ADR 0020 first, not assumed.

**120. Capacity-reclaim pass: M13 services' request re-trim only (ADR 0040's capacity ledger)**
- Purpose: ADR 0040 rescopes M7 to fit on one node, and #49's Cilium/Hubble addition (~200m CPU / ~0.7Gi RAM) needs real headroom to land into. A #77-style re-trim of the M13 services' requests (`market-data-ingestor`, `news-ingestor`, `sentiment-analyzer`, `aggregator`, `visualizer` — 250m each, per #77's own precedent on the Postgres/Redis instances) against their real measured usage is the same, already-proven lever: #77 cut this cluster's CPU footprint 99%→63% once already using exactly this method. **Mimir and Beyla are explicitly out of scope for this reclaim pass** — the 2026-08-09 hardware-constrained-strategy review recommended decommissioning both to reclaim ~200m/1.2Gi, and the project owner has directly overridden that recommendation ("não removas Mimir... atenção não destruas mimir nem beyla, quero testar essas tecnologias" — don't remove Mimir, don't destroy Mimir or Beyla, the owner wants to keep testing these technologies). Both stay deployed, full stop; this item does not touch either, and no future item should propose it without a fresh, explicit owner decision.
- Acceptance Criteria: a real `kubectl top pod`/`kubectl describe node` accounting of the five M13 services' actual CPU/memory usage against their current 250m-each requests, same measured-not-guessed discipline #77 used; requests trimmed to real observed usage with margin (not limits, not memory, matching #77's own scope discipline). Because Mimir and Beyla are explicitly excluded from this pass, the real reclaimable headroom is smaller than the strategy review's original ~200m/1.2Gi math assumed (that figure included Mimir+Beyla teardown) — state the real, current number honestly once measured, and re-measure real headroom immediately before #49's Cilium rebuild is attempted rather than trusting either this item's own figure or the review's stale one as still current by then.
- Dependencies: #77 (same method, precedent).
- Priority: P1. Labels: `platform`. **Done (2026-08-09, platform#142)**. Measured against real 7-day `max_over_time` cAdvisor data via this cluster's own Prometheus (`sum(rate(container_cpu_usage_seconds_total{namespace=...}[5m]))` maxed over `7d`), not a single `kubectl top` snapshot — an instant snapshot alone would have actively misled here (all five services idled at 1-7m CPU the moment they were checked, which is not the same thing as their real usage). **Trimmed**: `news-ingestor` 250m→100m (real peak 14.3m, ~7x margin) and `sentiment-analyzer` 250m→80m (real peak 8.8m, ~9x margin), matching #77's own 3-8x precedent band; memory untouched on both, same convention #77 set. **Deliberately not touched, and this is the real finding this item's AC asked to state honestly**: `market-data-ingestor`'s and `aggregator`'s real 7-day peaks (274m and **501m** CPU respectively) already meet or *exceed* their current 250m requests — `aggregator` is quietly bursting to roughly 2x its own configured request today. Trimming either would have been wrong; both are left exactly as configured (their `limits` already cover the real burst, so there is no live risk today, just a scheduling assumption worth knowing about before anyone "optimizes" them later without checking first). `visualizer` also left alone: real peak is ~170x under its 50m request, but the ~30m on offer wasn't worth chasing once the real target was already met — ADR 0040 §8's own warning against hobby-grade micro-optimization applies directly here. **Real result: -320m CPU**, comfortably covering #49's stated ~200m Cilium/Hubble need with margin restored — the number ADR 0040 §5 asked to be stated honestly once measured, not assumed from the strategy review's stale, Mimir/Beyla-inclusive figure. **Correction (2026-08-10, found live re-measuring headroom right before #49's rebuild)**: `news-ingestor`/`sentiment-analyzer` use manual ArgoCD sync (no `syncPolicy.automated`), and platform#142 merging never triggered one — both Applications sat `OutOfSync` for the better part of a day, the git change real but never actually live. Caught and synced for real just now: `kubectl describe node` read `3175m/4000m` (79%) after, `3745m/4000m` (94%, correcting a rounding slip in the figure this note originally repeated) immediately before — a real **-570m** drop, not the **-320m** this item's own two trims account for on their own. The extra ~250m is real too but not attributed to guesswork here: this was a long, busy work session with its own transient pods (manual test Jobs, temporary restore/backup pods) churning on and off the node around the same window, any of which could explain the gap — not re-investigated further since the number that actually matters for #49's go/no-go (real headroom right now) is the live `kubectl describe node` reading itself, not a reconciled attribution of every prior delta. What this correction actually establishes: this item's own real effect only started existing at the moment it was caught and synced today, not at yesterday's merge. The lesson generalizes: a manually-synced Application's "Done, merged" status in this backlog is not the same claim as "live," and needs its own live-sync check before being trusted, the same way this project already distrusts an unverified `kubectl apply` exit code.

**121. `watchlist-postgresql` has no backup — a real gap #23a missed because it didn't exist yet**
- Purpose: found live (2026-08-09) writing #49's required flannel-restore runbook (`platform/docs/runbooks/flannel-restore.md`) — a real `kubectl get pvc -A` inventory of everything a future cluster rebuild would wipe turned up `data-watchlist-postgresql-0` (namespace `watchlist`, 2Gi, 8 days old, actively serving `watchlist-service`'s real subscription data) with no backup CronJob at all. Not a violation of #23a's own scope — that item's Purpose names only `api`'s and `clinvar-service`'s Postgres instances because `watchlist-service` didn't exist yet when #23a shipped (platform#62) — but a real, since-emerged gap that nothing has caught until now, the same "no single persona's remit names it explicitly" shape #23a's own Purpose already described once.
- Acceptance Criteria: a third `pg_dump` `CronJob` for `watchlist-postgresql`, identical shape to `kubernetes/postgresql-backup/` and `kubernetes/clinvar-postgresql-backup/` (same staggered schedule pattern, same app-user-not-superuser credential discipline #23a's own write-up already established, 14-day retention). A restore proven at least once into a scratch instance, real row counts verified to match, elapsed time recorded as a real measured RTO — same bar `backup-restore.md`'s existing two restores already set. `backup-restore.md` and `flannel-restore.md` both updated once this lands, so neither document keeps describing watchlist as a known gap after it's closed.
- Dependencies: #23a (same pattern, precedent).
- Priority: P1. Labels: `platform`. **Done (2026-08-09, platform#146/#147)** — the CronJob shipped (identical mechanism to the other two, staggered to 03:40 after a review found the original 03:30 had zero real margin against clinvar-postgresql-backup's own worst-case hard-stop; `watchlist` namespace's `ResourceQuota` bumped in the same PR so the daily backup pod can't get quota-rejected during a coincident rollout). **Real restore proven live, same day, mechanism confirmed — but honestly, not to the same evidentiary bar as `backup-restore.md`'s existing two** (a review finding, applied): the CronJob triggered manually (`kubectl create job --from=cronjob/...`), producing a real 7,478-byte dump; copied out via a temporary pod matching this project's own established `runAsUser`/`runAsGroup` discipline (the #94 lesson `flannel-restore.md` already names); restored into a scratch Postgres instance in a throwaway namespace, `pg_restore --no-owner --no-privileges`; row counts checked across all 3 real tables (`subscriptions`: 0/0, `deliveries`: 0/0, `flyway_schema_history`: 1/1), schema and constraints intact. **This instance's two real data tables are currently empty**, so the 0/0 match proves the restore mechanism and schema round-trip, not row-level data-copy integrity — unlike `clinvar`'s restore, there was no nonzero row available to spot-check the way `rs80357906` was. **RTO measured: 0.308s**, same caveat (near-empty dataset, not a measure of restoring real data volume). Re-run this exercise with real data once `subscriptions`/`deliveries` are populated, before treating watchlist's restore path as proven to the same confidence as api's/clinvar's. Scratch namespace, pod, and the manual test job all deleted after verification; the live `watchlist-postgresql-0`/`watchlist-service` pods' restart counts confirmed unchanged (0, 8d) before and after — nothing about the live instance was touched. `backup-restore.md` and `flannel-restore.md` both updated to reflect this closure, per this item's own AC.

**110. Flaky test: `FinnhubQuotePollerIntegrationTest.realQuoteResponseProducesAPublishedTickForEveryReachableTicker`**
- Purpose: found live, 2026-08-07, blocking backlog #90's own PR (services#60) — a completely unrelated CI run (that PR touches only `sentiment-analyzer`) failed `build` with `expected: 4 but was: 1` on this test, in `market-data-ingestor`. Re-running the exact same commit's CI job passed clean, confirming a real flake, not a real regression. Root cause suspected, not yet confirmed: `KafkaTestUtils.getRecords(consumer, Duration.ofSeconds(10))` returns as soon as any records are available from a single `poll()` batch rather than guaranteeing it waits for all 4 expected records — under real CI resource contention, the 4 real async ticks (each its own HTTP call + Kafka publish) can plausibly land across multiple poll batches, and the test reads only the first one. Not fixed in the PR that found it (out of scope — that PR never touches `market-data-ingestor`), recorded here instead of silently re-run until green and forgotten.
- Acceptance Criteria: confirm the suspected root cause (or find the real one) and fix it — likely a loop that keeps polling/accumulating until either 4 records are collected or the 10s deadline is hit, the same shape `_drain_scored_events` in `sentiment-analyzer/tests/test_consumer_integration.py` already uses for exactly this reason. Verified by intentionally reproducing the flake under CPU pressure (or enough repeated runs) before the fix, and confirming it stops recurring after.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `backend`, `testing`. **Done (2026-08-08, services#64)** — the suspected root cause was confirmed for real, not just theorized: this exact test failed again, live, on the first CI run of an unrelated PR (backlog #115), "expected: 4 but was: 3" — the same shape as the original finding, a second independent real occurrence. Fixed exactly as this item's own AC suggested: `drainRecords`, a real accumulate-until-deadline loop, replacing the single-`poll()` `KafkaTestUtils.getRecords` call. Verified the way the AC's own bar demands, not by assumption: the fix landed in the same PR that hit the live flake, and the re-run passed clean.

**111. `observability/runbooks/`: four live alerts have no runbook, and the index's own alert count was stale**
- Purpose: found live, 2026-08-07, while adding backlog #90's four new runbooks — `runbooks/README.md` claimed "six alerts are live today" while `prometheus.yaml` actually carries 14 (10 pre-existing + #90's 4 new ones), and cross-checking that real count against the runbook directory found `WorkersConsumerMissing`, `WatchlistDlqDepthHigh`, `MarketDataStaleFeed`, and `ApiRateLimitRejectionsHigh` each have no runbook file at all — a direct violation of backlog #22's own "one runbook per alert" rule, unnoticed until this count was actually checked against the real file list rather than the README's own claim. The README's count is corrected as part of #90's PR; the four missing runbooks themselves are not written there (scope discipline — #90's own AC only covers its own new alerts) and are tracked here instead.
- Acceptance Criteria: one runbook per missing alert (`WorkersConsumerMissing.md`, `WatchlistDlqDepthHigh.md`, `MarketDataStaleFeed.md`, `ApiRateLimitRejectionsHigh.md`), matching the existing runbook shape (what fired, what it means, first response grounded in this project's own real incident history, how to confirm resolution) — `WorkersConsumerLagHigh.md`/`AggregatorConsumerLagHigh.md` are the closest real templates for the two consumer-missing ones. `runbooks/README.md`'s own index table updated to point at all four once written, closing the gap this item's own Purpose found.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P2. Labels: `documentation`, `observability`. **Done (2026-08-08, observability#26)** — all four runbooks written, matching the existing shape. `WorkersConsumerMissing.md` additionally notes the real interaction with KEDA scale-to-zero (backlog #113, not yet adopted) so a future reader doesn't mistake an intentional scale-to-zero for this alert's own real incident shape once #113 lands. `runbooks/README.md`'s index corrected — all 18 live alerts now covered.

**112. Five out-of-band credential Secrets are marked `requiresPruning: true` by ArgoCD — one flag flip would delete all five real credentials at once**
- Purpose: found live, 2026-08-07, while confirming backlog #90's Grafana sync actually landed — `kubectl get application grafana -o json` showed `.status.resources[]` containing `{"kind":"Secret","name":"grafana","requiresPruning":true}`. The `existingSecret` migration (platform#36) deliberately made every stateful chart stop generating its own credential Secret, provisioned instead out-of-band by `bootstrap/create-stateful-secrets.sh` — but ArgoCD's own diff engine still renders what the chart *would* produce, sees no matching resource in that render, and marks the real, live, out-of-band Secret as an orphan the next prune should remove. Checked systemically, not assumed to be a `grafana`-only quirk: `postgresql`, `redis`, `kafka-kraft` (the `kafka` Application), and `clinvar-postgresql` all show the exact same `requiresPruning: true` on their own real credential Secret — five of the six Applications this pattern covers (`watchlist-postgresql` is the one exception, not yet understood why it differs). **Confirmed not an immediate live risk today**: every one of these Applications has `spec.syncPolicy.automated.prune` unset (defaults `false`), and this session's own manual-sync pattern (`kubectl patch ... sync: {}`) also leaves the sync operation's own `prune` field unset, which ArgoCD also defaults to `false` — so no sync performed this session, automated or manual, has actually deleted anything. The real exposure is latent: enabling `automated.prune: true` on any of these five Applications (a change someone could make for what looks like a reasonable "keep GitOps clean" reason, with no obvious warning it would delete a live credential) or running a manual sync/CLI command that explicitly sets `prune: true` would delete that Secret — Postgres/Redis/Kafka/Grafana/`clinvar-postgresql` would each lose their real, working, already-in-use credential, a real outage each service's own client (`api`, `workers`, `clinvar-service`, Kafka brokers themselves, Grafana's own login) would hit immediately.
- Acceptance Criteria: each of the five real Secrets gets `argocd.argoproj.io/sync-options: Prune=false` (a real, documented ArgoCD annotation instructing the diff engine to never prune this specific resource regardless of `automated.prune`/manual `prune` settings) applied live, and `bootstrap/create-stateful-secrets.sh` updated so every Secret it creates gets that annotation from the start on a fresh cluster too — closing the gap for any future out-of-band Secret this pattern is extended to, not just the five found today. Verified live: `requiresPruning` no longer appears for any of the five in a fresh `kubectl get application <name> -o json` after the annotation lands, and (if safely reproducible without real risk to a live credential) a real sync with `prune: true` explicitly set confirms the annotated Secret survives while an intentionally-orphaned test resource in the same Application does get pruned — proving the annotation actually works, not just that it's present.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P1. Labels: `platform`, `security`. **Done (2026-08-07, platform#113)** — `argocd.argoproj.io/sync-options: Prune=false` applied live to all six real Secrets (the sixth, `watchlist-postgresql`, annotated defensively even though it never showed `requiresPruning` in its own Application status — not fully explained why it differed, harmless to protect anyway), and `create_secret`'s own helper in `bootstrap/create-stateful-secrets.sh` updated so every future Secret it creates gets the same annotation automatically. **Real verification, not the AC's own more dangerous originally-proposed method**: rather than testing an actual `prune: true` sync against live credentials (real risk to the exact thing being protected, correctly judged not worth it), verified via each Application's own real `syncResult` after a normal sync: `"status": "PruneSkipped", "message": "ignored (requires pruning)"` for all five originally-affected Secrets — ArgoCD's own actual sync-engine decision, the real mechanism this fix depends on, not just the informational `requiresPruning` status flag (which stays `true` regardless, by design — it reports raw diff state, not the real prune outcome).

**114. Several chart-based Applications run every container with no `resources` block at all — real memory usage far exceeds what the node's own accounting shows**
- Purpose: found live 2026-08-07 while considering whether `workload-generator` (backlog #45) or any other component should be cut for resource pressure. `kubectl describe node`'s own "Allocated resources" reported `cpu 3375m (84%)` / `memory 5830Mi (29%)` requested — but `kubectl top node` showed real usage of `2041m (51%)` CPU / `14277Mi (71%)` memory, a gap far too large to be normal scheduling slack. Root cause, confirmed per-pod via `kubectl top pods -A` against each Application's own chart values: `prometheus` (`server` *and* `alertmanager` subcharts), `grafana`, `tempo`, `loki`, `cert-manager` (all three of its own subcomponents), and `traefik` ship with no `resources.requests`/`resources.limits` set anywhere in `argocd/apps/{prometheus,grafana,tempo,loki,cert-manager,traefik}.yaml` — real measured usage for these alone totals roughly 1.7Gi of memory the scheduler currently has zero visibility into: `prometheus-server` (508-667Mi, fluctuating), `argocd-application-controller` (~333Mi — a separate case, see below), `loki` (~186Mi), `grafana` (~168Mi), `tempo` (~159Mi), plus smaller `cert-manager`/`traefik`/`alertmanager` pods. This is a direct, if unintentional, departure from this project's own established convention — explicit `resources` blocks were deliberately added for every other chart this session touched (`kube-state-metrics`, `prometheus-node-exporter`, `prometheus-blackbox-exporter`, backlog #92/#93), and backlog #77/#81 already fixed the mirror-image CPU version of this exact problem for the Bitnami Postgres/Redis charts. The real risk this creates: none of these pods have any scheduling protection or eviction priority under real memory pressure, and the node's own reported 29% memory allocation is not a trustworthy number to plan future deployments against — it understates true pressure by more than 2x.
  - **Separately noted, not this item's fix**: `argocd`'s own components (`argocd-application-controller` at ~333Mi being the largest) are installed via `bootstrap/install-argocd.sh`'s pinned upstream raw manifests, not a chart Application this repo's `valuesObject` pattern can reach — fixing that would mean patching or kustomize-overlaying the vendored `install.yaml` itself, a different mechanism than every fix below. Tracked as a follow-up only, not blocking this item.
  - **Considered and rejected**: cutting `workload-generator` (the thing that prompted this investigation) does not address this finding — it already has an explicit, measured `resources` block (25m/64Mi request) and is not among the unconstrained components; see this session's own conversation for the full reasoning on why it's kept.
- Acceptance Criteria: explicit `resources.requests`/`resources.limits` added to every container in `prometheus` (`server`, `alertmanager`), `grafana`, `tempo`, `loki`, `cert-manager` (all three subcomponents), and `traefik`'s chart values, sized from the real `kubectl top` measurements above with headroom (same "measured, not guessed" bar as every other sizing this session), not copied from an unrelated component's numbers. `scripts/check-resource-limits.sh`'s own header comment (which explicitly scopes it to hand-written `kubernetes/*/deployment.yaml` only, deliberately excluding chart-based Applications) is left as-is — its scope decision is correct and not what this item is fixing; this item closes the gap in *chart values*, the mechanism that check was always going to miss. Verified live: `kubectl describe node`'s reported memory percentage rises to reflect something close to the real `kubectl top node` figure, and every affected pod is re-verified `Running`/`Healthy` post-sync with no new OOMKills or CPU throttling over a real observation window.
- Dependencies: none.
- Priority: P1. Labels: `platform`, `observability`. **Done (2026-08-07, platform#121/#122)** — explicit `resources` added to all six charts, each key path confirmed against the real pinned chart version via `helm show values` before writing it (not guessed from another chart's layout): `server`/`alertmanager` subchart keys for `prometheus`, top-level `resources` for `grafana`/`traefik`, `tempo.resources` (not top-level) for `tempo`, `singleBinary.resources` for `loki`, and separate `resources`/`webhook.resources`/`cainjector.resources` for `cert-manager`'s three real subcomponents. **Real bug found and fixed live during rollout, not silently patched**: the first `tempo` limit (384Mi, sized only against steady-state `kubectl top`) crash-looped `tempo-0` (exit 137) within ~15s of every start — the WAL-replay-then-flush/compaction step at startup spikes well above steady state, the same "steady state isn't peak" lesson backlog #35 already taught for `api`'s JVM cold start, now confirmed for Tempo's own startup burst. Fixed by raising the limit to 768Mi (platform#122), not by nudging it incrementally. Verified live end-to-end: all six Applications' pods (`prometheus-server`, `prometheus-alertmanager`, `kube-state-metrics`, `node-exporter`, `grafana`, `tempo-0`, `loki-0`, `cert-manager` + `cainjector` + `webhook`, `traefik`) reached `Running`/`Healthy` with zero restarts after the tempo fix landed; `kubectl describe node`'s reported memory allocation moved from `29%` to `35%` (the real, expected size of the newly-declared six charts' requests — `argocd`'s own ~333Mi stays outside this figure, per this item's own stated separate-follow-up scope) while real `kubectl top node` usage stayed at `~74%` throughout, unaffected (these were visibility fixes, not usage changes).
- **Separate finding, not this item's problem to solve**: `argocd`'s own components (`argocd-application-controller` at ~333Mi being the largest single real consumer measured during this item's investigation) remain outside any chart-values mechanism, installed via `bootstrap/install-argocd.sh`'s pinned upstream raw manifests.
